This concludes the eighth chapter, on the sequence of the sites, from the Abhidhāna, the Highest Tantra.

Then, further, I shall explain
The rite of the solitary hero.
The mantra practitioner visualizes at the navel,
In the center of wind and fire,
Upon that a multicolored lotus,
Upon that a sun maṇḍala,

And upon that a skull cup filled with the nectar of the five wisdoms. In its center the vowels and consonants are doubled, arranged in forward order and in reverse order. From the union of Vajrasattva blessed by hūṃ, arisen from joy and delight, one meditates on glorious Heruka, the embodiment of compassion, whirling with passion.

He has four faces and twelve arms, and stands with the right leg extended. His fangs are bared, he has three eyes, his face is contorted, and he wears a garland of skulls. His body is sealed with the six mudrās of the hero. He presses down the heads and breasts of Mahābhairava and Kālarātri. He wears a tiger skin and is suffused with the flavor of compassion. The two hands that embrace Vajravārāhī hold a nine-pronged vajra and a vajra bell. Two other hands hold the stretched-out skin of Gaṇapati. The third right hand holds a vajra spear, the fourth a hook, the fifth a vajra curved knife, and the sixth a vajra ḍamaru. The third left hand holds a skull cup filled with marrow, held for the offering of yoga, and a vajra khaṭvāṅga, topped with a five-pronged vajra, with a most terrifying vajra bell hanging from it and various banners hanging down, marked in the middle with a crossed vajra, with a single-pointed vajra at its lower end. The fourth holds a vajra noose, the fifth the head of Brahmā, and the sixth an axe. His faces are blue, yellow, red and green, utterly terrifying, and he abides in erotic grace, laughter and majesty.

In front of him, arisen from the vowels and consonants, the Blessed Lady Vajravārāhī sits embracing him. Her body is red; she has four arms, four faces and three eyes, and her hair hangs loose. She is naked, with a girdle of pieces of bone. In her left hand is a skull cup fully filled with the mind of awakening and with the blood of the wicked māras; with her right, making the threatening gesture, she holds a vajra curved knife; her two other hands hold a ḍamaru and a khaṭvāṅga. She has the splendor of the fire at the end of the eon; her mouth drips with blood, and she delights in blood. Her two thighs are evenly crossed. She is the embodiment of the compassion of great bliss. Her faces are red, green, yellow and blue, and she acts wicked and terrifying. Her hands and body color should be drawn in accordance with the faces and body color of the Blessed One. Upon this, the supreme yogin sets out the deities in the sequence of the inner sites and the rest.

bu: Khaṇḍakapāla and Pracaṇḍā, at the head. jā: Mahākaṅkāla and Caṇḍākṣī, at the crown of the head. au: Kaṅkāla and Prabhāvatī, at the right ear. a: Vikaṭadaṃṣṭra and Mahānāsā, at the back of the crown; these are the sites. go: Suravairin and Vīramatī, at the left ear. rā: between the eyebrows, Amitābha and Kharvarī. de: at the two eyes, Vajraprabha and Laṅkeśvarī. mā: at the shoulders, Vajradeha and Drumacchāyā; these are the secondary sites. This is the maṇḍala of mind, those who move in the sky. kā: in the two armpits, Aṅkurika and Airāvatī. o: at the two breasts, Vajrajaṭila and Mahābhairavā; these are the fields. tri: in the center of the navel, Mahābala and Vāyuvegā. ko: at the tip of the nose, Vajra Hūṃkāra and Surābhakṣī; these are the secondary fields. kā: at the mouth, Subhadra and the goddess Śyāmā. la: at the throat, Vajraprabha and Subhadrā; these are the chandohas. kā: at the heart, Vajrabhairava and Hayakarṇā.

hi: at the anus, Virūpākṣa and Khagānanā; these are the secondary chandohas. This is the wheel of speech, those who move on the earth. pre: at the genitals, Mahābala and Cakravegā. gṛ: at the rectum, Vajraratna and Khaṇḍarohā; these are the meeting places. sau: at the two thighs, Hayagrīva and Śauṇḍinī. su: at the two shanks, Ākāśagarbha and Cakravarmiṇī; these are the secondary meeting places. nā: at the toes, glorious Heruka and Suvīrā. si: at the two soles, Padmanarteśvara and Mahābalā; these are the charnel grounds. ma: at the two big toes, Vairocana and Cakravartinī. ku: at the two knees, Vajrasattva and Mahāvīryā; these are the secondary charnel grounds. This is the wheel of body, those who dwell beneath the earth.

The Ḍākinī holds a vajra; Lāmā, a bell; Khaṇḍarohā and Rūpiṇī, the fist that holds an elephant skin. Kākāsyā holds a vajra spear; Ulūkāsyā, a hook; Śvānāsyā, a curved knife; Śūkarāsyā, a ḍamaru. Yamadāhī bears a skull khaṭvāṅga; Yamadūtī, a noose; Yamadaṃṣṭrī, the head of Brahmā; Yamamathanī, an axe. The vessel of the mind of awakening is the essence of the four faces.

The earth element is Pātanī; the water element is Māraṇī; the fire element is Ākarṣaṇī; the wind element is Narteśvarī; the space element is Padmajālinī. The aggregate of form is Vairocana; the aggregate of feeling is Vajra Sūrya; the aggregate of perception is Padmanarteśvara; the aggregate of formations is Vajrarāja; the aggregate of consciousness is Vajrasattva; and the suchness of all the tathāgatas is glorious Vajra Heruka. The two eyes are the vajra of delusion; the two ears are the vajra of aversion; the nose is the vajra of avarice; the mouth is the vajra of attachment; the tangible is the vajra of envy; all the sense sources are the vajra of mastery. Mind is Akṣobhya, speech is Amitāyus, and body is Vairocana.

oṃ ha at the heart, Vajrasattva. namaḥ hi at the head, Vairocana. svāhā hu at the crown of the head, Padmanarteśvara. bau ṣaṭ he at the two shoulders, Heruka. hūṃ hūṃ ho at the two eyes, Vajra Sūrya. phaṭ haṃ on all the limbs, the armor of Paramāśva. oṃ vaṃ at the navel, Vajravārāhī. haṃ yoṃ at the heart, Yāminī. hriṃ moṃ at the mouth, Mohanī. hreṃ hrīṃ at the head, Sañcālinī. hūṃ hūṃ at the crown of the head, Santrāsanī. phaṭ phaṭ Caṇḍikā, the armor of all the limbs.

Knotting the index fingers, join the middle fingers firmly;
Join the thumbs firmly as a vajra.
Having set it in the center of the forehead,
Turning and turning, one should circle it all around.
Pressing down with the feet and looking upward,
Proclaiming upward the sound pheṃ,
Summon the heroes and yoginīs
Who dwell in the world systems of the ten directions.

jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥ Through the application of the vajra hook and the rest, summon them, cause them to enter, bind them and bring them under control. Seeing those heroes and yoginīs completely fill the expanse of the sky, offer the empowerment with a skull cup fully filled with the five nectars, together with the proper posture and method.

Just as, at the very moment of his birth,
All the tathāgatas bathed him,
So, with the pure water of the gods,
I shall likewise perform the bathing.

oṃ sarva tathāgata ābhiṣekata samaya śriye hūṃ hūṃ

Upon a maṇḍala of wind and fire is a skull cup. There, the syllable a is generated, and it is fully filled with the nectar of the five wisdoms. Upon the moon of sixteen digits it is blessed by the syllables hūṃ, āḥ and oṃ, and one meditates that from the emanation of the three syllables the three wheels arise. oṃ samaya śuddhāḥ sarva dharmāḥ smaya śuddho'haṃ One imagines the essence of all phenomena abiding in sameness.

Through the application of the five wisdoms
One drinks the water of vajra nectar.

oṃ vajra amṛta udaka hūṃ ṭha ṭha traṃ a khaṃ hūṃ oṃ

Through the application of the recitation of wisdom,
Send forth the supreme wheel.
Having taken a rosary of great conch,
Then one should recite with it.
Having performed one's own breath control,
By meditating, it blazes at once.

oṃ vajra śrī he he ru ru kaṃ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ ṭākinī jāla sambaraṃ svāhā oṃ hrīḥ h ha huṃ hūṃ phaṭ These are the heart mantra and the near-heart mantra. oṃ vajra vairotanīye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ sarva buddha ḍākinīye vajra varṇṇanīye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā These are the heart mantra and the near-heart mantra of the goddess.

If one has not become weary,
One should recite thus and meditate.
If one has become weary, meditate on the purifications.

Now, what are the thirty-seven factors of awakening? The close placement of mindfulness on the body is the Ḍākinī. The close placement of mindfulness on feelings is Lāmā. The close placement of mindfulness on phenomena is Khaṇḍarohā. The close placement of mindfulness on mind is Rūpiṇī. The basis of miraculous power that is intention is Pracaṇḍā. The basis of miraculous power that is diligence is Caṇḍākṣī. The basis of miraculous power that is investigation is Prabhāvatī. The basis of miraculous power that is mind is Mahānāsā. This is the purification of the lotus of wisdom. The faculty of faith is Vīramatī. The faculty of diligence is Kharvarī. The faculty of mindfulness is Laṅkeśvarī. The faculty of meditative absorption is Drumacchāyā. This is the purification of the wheel of mind. The faculty of wisdom is Airāvatī. The power of faith is Mahābhairavā. The power of diligence is Vāyuvegā. The power of mindfulness is Surābhakṣī. The power of meditative absorption is the goddess Śyāmā. The power of wisdom is Subhadrā. The awakening factor of meditative absorption is Hayakarṇā. The awakening factor of diligence is Kākāsyā. This is the purification of the wheel of speech. The awakening factor of joy is Cakravegā. The awakening factor of pliancy is Khaṇḍarohā. The awakening factor of discernment of phenomena is Śauṇḍinī. The awakening factor of mindfulness is Cakravarmiṇī. The awakening factor of equanimity is Suvīrā.

Right view is Mahābalā. Right thought is Cakravartinī. Right speech is Mahāvīryā. This is the purification of the wheel of body. Right action is Kākāsyā. Right livelihood is Ulūkāsyā. Right effort is Śvānāsyā. Right mindfulness is Śūkarāsyā. These are the doors of the four liberations. Right meditative absorption is the Blessed One, glorious Heruka: the very nature of the vajra of the wisdom of the utterly pure dharmadhātu is none other than myself. Generating the virtuous phenomena that have not arisen is Yamadāhī. Fully guarding the virtuous phenomena that have arisen is Yamadūtī. Fully abandoning the nonvirtuous phenomena that have arisen is Yamadaṃṣṭrī. Not generating the nonvirtuous phenomena that have not arisen is Yamamathanī. This is the purification of the wheel of samaya.

If one meditates in this way, leaving nothing out,
The practitioner will certainly attain accomplishment.
For the sake of beings, the yogin himself
Is sameness, the sacred samaya.
For each of the five wheels
Meditate on the distinct divisions of the rite.
The distinct divisions of the womb-born
Have the supremacy of the five wheels.
Meditating chiefly on the yoginīs and the rest:
These are the divisions of the distinct divisions.
Just as Vajra Heruka,
So too, in the center of the goddess,
Is the union with Buddhaḍākinī,
And likewise the retinue of yoginīs.
Oneself as the hero of vajra body
Visualize in the center of the bhaga.
As in the sequence of meditation,
So join them in the three wheels.
I myself meditate on the five bhagas:
In the center, union with the Ḍākinī,
Union with Padmaḍākinī,
And, distinguishing Vajraḍākinī,
The three wheels in the center of the bhaga,
Meditate on Heruka as the supreme.
When meditating on the wheel of samaya:
The maṇḍala of the samaya ḍākinīs,
The maṇḍala of the wisdom ḍākinīs,
The maṇḍala of the wisdom ḍākinīs;
In the center of the multicolored lotus and the rest
Are the applications of the wheel of wisdom.
That, here, in the centers of the bhaga,
Meditate on in the five wheels.
The solitary hero Heruka,
And likewise Vārāhī as well:
Body color, faces, hands and emblems,
Visualize them on a multicolored lotus.
In the center of that very lotus
Meditate on Heruka as the supreme.
Together with the skull cup, equally,
Joining the divisions of the five,
Meditate on the sequence of the yoginīs;
Visualize them in the center of the bhaga.
The lord of the wheel has not been seen.
With the mudrā of embrace,
Imagine the wheel of heroes there.
Visualize each one's own yoginī
Hidden in the heart.
The yoga of the feast gathering
Is beautiful with four arms:
Two are the embracing arms,
The others hold various emblems.
Two hands embrace,
And in the center of her bhaga
The hero's meditation is not seen.
The application hidden in the relative:
The supreme meditation too is not seen.
The meditation of the egg-born
That applies the sequence
Is the thought of the five gathered together:
This is the meditation of the supreme egg-born.
Through the divisions of the divisions of the wheel,
Thus the yogin meditates.
According to the particular intention,
So one meditates on what is desired.
Through the divisions of the divisions of the lord,
Meditate on the divisions of the family.
Through the divisions of the divisions of the goddess,
Meditate on the lord as well.
Through the divisions of the divisions of the names,
Apply them to the word "ḍākinī."
Or else, by whichever division of the three bodies,
Whatever is to be made the lord:
Through the divisions of the lord and the rest,
These are the divisions of the divisions of the wheel.
Through all the divisions of the family,
Distinguish the heroes and the yoginīs.
Through the application of the songs of the womb-born
Examine the meditations thoroughly.
The egg-born has no song:
It has abandoned sound and the fading of sound.
This is the self-arisen Blessed One,
The sacred, supreme bliss of yoga.
Some desire yoga;
Some do not desire yoga:
Having abandoned song, sound and the rest,
They abide in the state of no thing,
Subtle, inexhaustible, without any thing,
Free of grasped and grasper.
The enjoyment of the supreme state
Is together with song, sound and the rest.
Having abandoned song, sound and the rest,
They abide in the state of no thing,
Subtle, inexhaustible, without any thing,
Free of grasped and grasper.
The enjoyment of the supreme state
Is together with song, sound and the rest.
All the qualities of desire, too,
I shall consume with benefit and loving care.
Through striving for the welfare of beings,
Patience and diligence are supreme and sacred.
The yogin does not disparage song,
And the ḍākinī delights in what benefits.

ho Lord who has found bliss in the three existences!
a la la la ho
Victor possessed of bliss, assembly of garlands,
The vajra plays in the lotus!
a la la la ho
Sugata, released from the laughter of separation,
Lord, essence of all delight,
Delighting utterly with passion,
Essence of the assembly in the three existences!
a la la ho
You who are fully liberated through delight,
Liberate me completely from desire!

When the yogin of the lotus,
Arisen from the vowels,
Has sung this king of songs,
The bliss of vajra yoga
Rises from the net of lotuses.
Urged by this, the protectors,
For the bliss of great bliss, from the celestial palace,
All the buddhas, arise and are born,
And all the victors without exception come to birth.
Together with song, there is no thing.
For the one who has abandoned the egg-born portion,
Its form is not seen:
There is no bliss and no suffering,
It is nondual and without any thing.
Examine the domain of examination.
Liberated from dharma and from what is not dharma,
One is not stained by evil or fault.
For the one liberated as nondual in all things,
The egg-born is together with a thing.
Since one is woken from sleep by song,
The deluded should not disparage the wise.
For the sake of those who have not understood, and of what is to be understood,
For the welfare of beings there are buddhas.

The field-born and the innate goddesses,
Likewise the place-born yoginīs,
The family-born goddesses, the born goddesses,
Likewise the invisible sky-goers,
The last birth is supreme indeed;
The middle is various and excellent.
The thirty move upon the earth;
Those who move beneath the earth dwell below.
Yakṣiṇīs and nāginīs,
Kiṃnarīs and gandharvīs,
All the goddesses accomplished before,
And others too whose intent is on the Sugata,
Who delight in the Three Jewels:
All of them are the wheel of the outer maṇḍala.
Making use of various songs,
One should always sing songs.
With the bliss of various flavors,
Worship the goddesses and the lord of the ḍākinīs.
Abiding in the maṇḍala of wind,
Meditate on the wheel of body.
The wise one, firmly established in water,
Meditates on the maṇḍala of speech.
Abiding in the maṇḍala of fire,
The maṇḍala of mind is great bliss.
Abiding in the maṇḍala of Mahendra, the earth,
Is the supreme and excellent maṇḍala of samaya.
That very maṇḍala of wisdom:
Contemplate it and meditate on it.
In the center of the maṇḍala of the bhaga,
Meditate on a multicolored lotus.
In the center of the maṇḍala of the sun,
Meditate on the solitary hero.
A wheel, a lotus, a garland of vajras:
These three wheels encircle it all around,
Just as the supreme wheel of samaya
Is encircled by a garland of crossed vajras.
Likewise, with the five kinds of hungry ghosts,
Encircle the outer maṇḍala.
Outside are the fierce charnel grounds,
Of various colors, supreme and excellent,
With various trees, delightful,
Dogs and jackals, terrifying,
Crows, owls and herons,
Vultures and sipikas.
Likewise the cremation grounds,
With skeletons mounted on stakes,
And charred remains hanging all about;
Likewise the half-burned and the unburned,
Heads, skulls and severed parts,
Torsos, hands and heads,
Jackals giving voice to their howls,
And snakes of every kind dwelling there in delight.
Lions, boars and tigers,
Goats and sheep likewise,
Elephants, dogs, donkeys and oxen,
And camels: various, the supreme of bliss.
Food of various kinds,
The finest drinks of liquor,
Supreme gatherings of heroes,
And yoginīs, various and supreme,
Holding skull cups and khaṭvāṅgas,
Curved knives and the finest hand drums,
Various drums of the gods,
Foods of supreme flavor of every kind,
Various kisses, ālikā,
And the supreme and excellent kuśale.
In such a charnel ground
One should give torma
To yakṣas, vetālas and rākṣasas,
And meditate on the forms of Heruka.
With hand drum, vajra and bell,
And cymbals, perform the dances.
Clad in the directions, joining the mudrā,
Uttering hūṃ phaṭ and loud cries,
In the posture with the right leg extended,
Having bound the blazing mudrā,
And having filled the mouth with the samaya,
Here one should call to mind the secret mantra:
oṃ āḥ ārali ho jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥ vajraḍākinī samaya stvaṃ dṛśya ho
This should be recited three, four or five times,
Again and again.
In forward order and in reverse,
Give to them also in the skull cup.
Having opened the vajra palms upward,
Here one should apply the secret mantra:
oṃ kha kha khāhi khāhi sarba yakṣa rākṣasa bhāta preta piśāca unmāda apasmāra ḍākaḍākinyādayaḥ imaṃ baliṃ gṛhṇaṃtu samaya rakṣantu mama sarba siddhimme prayacchantu yathaibaṃ yatheṣṭaṃ bhuñjatha pibatha jighatha mātikramatha mama sarba kāratayasaṅ sukha biśuddhaye sahāyikaṃ bhabantu huṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ svāhā
By the sequence of this rite,
It should be given at the four junctures of the sessions.
Dwelling alone and at ease,
The purpose of the mahāmāṃsa, the great flesh, is taught.
The yogin, resting in meditative equipoise,
Should here eat all of it.

From the Highest of the Highest of the Tantra of Discourse, this is the ninth chapter, the teaching that distinguishes the stages of the nondual wheel.

Then, furthermore, I shall explain
The rite of the pure abode.
The secret of the secret rite
Is the supreme heart of the ḍākinīs.
The union that is the abode of all secret mantras
Is what grants every accomplishment.

Then the vajra retinue of the Great Lord over all the tathāgatas, the great ḍākas together with their messengers, the hosts of ḍākinīs, and also the other great Blessed Ones, the Samantabhadra vajra holders, bowed to their own lord and petitioned him:

"How wonderful! You, being of bliss!
How wonderful! O lord of bliss,
The supreme secret of the ḍākinīs,
Your own maṇḍala: please explain it.
O Buddha yoga, great yoga,
Vajra yoga become supreme,
Speak your own secret maṇḍala,
From which the bliss of the three worlds arises.
O Vajraratna, great Lotus,
Various union and various dance,
Speak your own pure maṇḍala."

Arising from Vajravairocana,
The palace is four-cornered and most beautiful,
Fully endowed with four doors,
And complete with every ornament.
This maṇḍala arisen from bliss
Has in its center two wheels,
The supreme wheels of mind and speech,
Eight-spoked, blue and red,
Encircled by vajras and lotuses.
Draw it encircled outside by a wheel.
Outside the maṇḍala, the supreme maṇḍala,
Various yoginīs
Sport with song and dance.
Outside, a garland of hungry ghosts,
Of various colors, supreme and excellent,
Dance with dances of the hands,
Their faces contorted and their tongues waggling.
Its center has become empty;
Upon a moon is the supreme syllable hūṃ.
It is pervaded by a vajra:
Meditate on Vajrasattva,
With three faces and six arms,
The three bodies, possessed of the mood of compassion,
Fully joined with the great mudrā,
Seated in the vajra cross-legged posture
Upon a seat of a multicolored lotus
In the center of a moon disk.
His faces are white, blue and saffron.
He holds the vajra of body, speech and mind,
The skull cup and the khaṭvāṅga,
Embraces with vajra and bell,
Holds the hand drum and the head of Brahmā,
And wears Akṣobhya as his crown.
The places of the ḍākinīs should be laid out,
And the heroes likewise should be laid out.
The elements, aggregates and sense sources
Should be meditated on as body, speech and mind.
The goddess too has a form like his;
She is the Great Seal, bliss.
At the heart, the seed maṃ
In the center of a moon disk,
Together with two ho syllables:
First calling to mind the accomplishment,
Seal it with this mudrā.
Immediately, in the secret lotus
At the heart of the goddess,
Having engaged in passion,
The great Protector, through passion,
With the great bliss of Vajrasattva,
Sports together with her,
And the two organs are joined.
The minds of the heroes and yoginīs
Having become joyful,
When with joyful intent
They urge him with the sound of hand drums,
He rises from the joy of sameness.
With the vowels and consonants joined in sameness,
Great bliss was delighted.

Then all the tathāgatas, equal in number to the atoms of everything reaching to the limits of space and of the dharmadhātu, together with their retinues, pervaded by the secret heroes together with the ḍākinīs, and led by a great host of bodhisattvas, and all the hosts of ḍākas and ḍākinīs without exception or remainder, and the vast hosts of messengers and female messengers who are the nature of the outer consorts, were amazed, gazed with their eyes, and embraced one another equally.

With the magical display of dance,
Rising up in great joy,
Joining their palms at the crown of the head,
Delighted by the blessing,
They should urge the great Protector
With these songs of delight:

"How wonderful! The wondrous, marvelous Dharma!
How wonderful! The wondrous, marvelous way!
How wonderful! The wondrous, marvelous secret!
How wonderful! Arisen from bliss!
How wonderful! The meaning of Samantabhadra!
How wonderful! Bliss truly gathered!
How wonderful! Great bliss, pure!
How wonderful! Bliss, the supreme of bliss!
How wonderful! The union of secret mantra!
How wonderful! The conduct of the mind!
How wonderful! Endowed with the seal of sameness!
Oh wonder! Accomplishment truly arises!
Oh wonder! Through the perfections!
Oh wonder! Passion following the consort!
Oh wonder! Great action of every kind!
Oh wonder! The threefold union, pure!
Oh wonder! The vajra joined in sameness!
Oh wonder! The lotus opening!
Oh wonder! The two organs joined as one!
Oh wonder! The marvelous sameness!
Oh wonder! The great pure dance!
Oh wonder! The stainless song, how excellent!
Oh wonder! The music well played!
Oh wonder! Dispelling darkness with phaṭ!
Oh wonder! Flowers and beautiful garments!
Oh wonder! Incense, the lovely ghati!
Oh wonder! Lamps dispelling delusion!
Oh wonder! Supreme perfume, cool!
Oh wonder! The yoga of union!
Oh wonder! Illusion like space!
Oh wonder! Buddha from the beginning, vajra from the beginning!
Oh wonder! Jewel from the beginning, bestowing bliss!
Oh wonder! Lotus from the beginning, drawn from the beginning!
Oh wonder! Various illusion, magically displayed!
Oh wonder! Wondrous, marvelous Buddha!
Oh wonder! Wondrous, this itself is marvelous!
Oh wonder! Wondrous are sentient beings!
Oh wonder! Wondrous, they are the goddess!"

Released by this, the protectors:
From the seed of supreme union
Arises one the color of saffron,
Marked with a vajra,
With six faces and twelve arms,
Adorned by Vārāhī.
The great hero Mañjuvajra
Abides in the half cross-legged posture,
Three-eyed and laughing aloud,
Fierce, terrifying and menacing,
Possessed of the dance moods, compassion among them.
Bhairava and Kālarātri
He keeps pressed beneath his feet.
Or else, standing with the right leg extended,
He is born as the great source of yoga.
In the center of a moon disk
He is seated on a multicolored lotus.
He has the color of fresh saffron stamens
And is adorned by that mudrā.
His faces are blue, green and yellow,
Red and white, and peaceful.
He has a crown ornament of a garland of skulls
And is beautified by buddha forms.
He holds vajra and bell together,
Squeezes the breasts of the goddess,
Wears the brahmanical thread,
And remains with the skin of fierce Bhairava
Bound about his waist.
He holds the skull cup and the khaṭvāṅga,
Carries a sword and an utpala petal,
A hook, a noose, a hand drum,
A head, and likewise a bow.

With body color and hand emblems like his,
The great Vajravārāhī
Abides in the state of great passion,
Entwining him well with her two thighs.
Great joy, enchanting,
Her body adorned with a garland of heads,
She is marked with the six mudrās.
In this way the mantrin should meditate:
Mañjuvajra, great bliss.
By joining the places and the rest to their lords,
All the lords, wisdom and the rest,
It is the union of the net of the ḍākinīs;
It is the vow, the supreme of vows.
First is the wheel of mind.

On the eastern spoke, in Pullīramalaya, are Khaṇḍakapāla and Pracaṇḍā: white, blue and green. In the north, in Jālandhara, are Mahākaṅkāla and Caṇḍākṣī: yellow, blue and green. In the west, in Oḍiyāna, are Kaṅkāla and Prabhāvatī: red, blue and green. In the south, in Arbuda, are Vikaṭadaṃṣṭra and Mahānāsā: green, yellow and red. In the southeast, the direction of fire, in Godāvarī, are Suravairin and Vīramatī: red, yellow and green. In the southwest, the direction of Nairṛti, in Rāmeśvara, are Amitābha and Kharvarī: white, yellow and green. In the northwest, the direction of wind, in Devīkoṭa, are Vajraprabha and Laṅkeśvarī: the color of the sky, yellow and green. In the northeast, the direction of Īśāna, in Mālava, are Vajradeha and Drumacchāyā: green, yellow and blue. This is the wheel of mind: the heroes Khaṇḍakapāla and the rest, holding vajra and bell, embrace Pracaṇḍā and the rest.

Second, in the wheel of speech: on the eastern spoke, in Kāmarūpa, are Aṅkurika and Airāvatī: white, blue and green. In the north, in Oḍra, are Vajrajaṭila and Mahābhairavā: green, yellow and blue. In the west, in Triśakuni, are Mahāvīra and Vāyuvegā: blue, yellow and green. In the south, in Kosala, are Vajra Hūṃkāra and Surābhakṣī: red, yellow and green. In the southeast, the direction of fire, in Kaliṅga, are Subhadra and Śyāmādevī: green, yellow and red. In the southwest, in Lampāka, are Vajraprabha and Subhadrā: yellow, blue and green. In the northwest, the direction of wind, in Kāñcī, are Mahābhairava and Hayakarṇā: smoke-colored, yellow and blue. In the northeast, the direction of Īśāna, in Himālaya, are Virūpākṣa and Khagānanā: the color of the sky, yellow and green. This is the wheel of speech: Aṅkurika and the rest, holding lotus and lotus bell, embrace Airāvatī and the rest. Thus these are the two wheels, of those who move in the sky and of those who move on the earth.

The third, the body, arising from the vajra, is four-cornered with four gates. At the eastern gate, in Pretapurī, are Mahābala and Cakravegā: blue, yellow and green. At the northern gate, in Gṛhadevatā, are Vajraratna and Khaṇḍarohā: yellow, blue and green. At the western gate, in Saurāṣṭra, are Hayagrīva and Śauṇḍinī: red, yellow and green. At the southern gate, in Suvarṇadvīpa, are Ākāśagarbha and Cakravarmiṇī: white, blue and green. In the southeast, the direction of fire, in Nagara, are Heruka and Suvīrā: the color of the sky, yellow, blue and green. In the southwest, in Sindhu, are Padmanarteśvara and Mahābalā: yellow, blue and green. In the northwest, the direction of wind, in Maru, are Vairocana and Cakravartinī: white, blue and green. In the northeast, in Kulatā, are Vajrasattva and Mahāvīryā: white, blue and green. In the wheel of body, Mahābala and the rest are embraced by Cakravegā and the rest, with wheel and wheel bell.

All these heroes stand with the right leg extended. They are three-eyed, three-faced and six-armed, holding a skull cup and a khaṭvāṅga, a hand drum and a curved knife. Just as with the arms and hand emblems of the heroes, so it is with the ḍākinīs as well:

They have crown ornaments of garlands of skulls,
And are adorned by the lord of their own family.
All of them bear the five mudrās
And wear tiger skins as garments.
The goddesses, with their hair unbound,
Entwine well with their two thighs.
All of them are clad in the directions,
Their bodies transformed into terrifying forms.
In its center is Mañjuvajra,
The supreme one who bears the half moon,
Treading upon a crossed vajra.

Vārāhī, with her hair unbound,
Is naked and wears a girdle.
Having summoned the wheel of wisdom,
Following the sequence of the earlier rite,
Draw it in and meditate on it dissolving.
Bless it through the four acts.
All the heroes and yoginīs
Should be laid out in the sequence of the places and the rest.
The elements, aggregates and sense sources
Are the excellence of body, speech and mind.
The essence, become the supreme wisdom:
Empowerment too should be conferred.
Then the armor should be put in place.
The wise one's recitation of wisdom:
Restraining the breath, one recites well,
And through meditating on what is meditated keeps it secret.
oṃ āḥ maṃ hūṃ
Through the practice of sending forth and drawing in,
Recite also the mantra of the nondual garland.
Then the samaya being
Should be recited according to the rite:
oṃ śrī mañju bajṛdtrakāḥ hūṃ phaṭ
ḍākinī jāla samabara hūṃ phaṭ svāhā
oṃ bajra bārāhi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā
oṃ kara kara khaṇḍa kapālini huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ pracaṇḍe huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ kuru kuru mahā kaṃkāla huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ caṇḍākṣi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ bandha bandha he kaṃkāla huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ prabhabati huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ trāsaya trāsaya bikaṭa daṃṣḍiṇi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ mahānāse huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ kṣobhaya kṣobhaya subairiṇi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ bīramati huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ hrauṃ hrauṃ amitābha huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ kharbari huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ hraḥ hraḥ bajra prabha huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ laṅkeśvari huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ pheṃ pheṃ bajra ṅeha huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ drumācchāye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ phaṭ phaṭ aṅkurika huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ airābati huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ daha daha bajra jāṭila huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ mahā bhairabi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ paca paca mahā bīra huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ bāyu bege huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ bhakṣa bhakṣa bajra hūṃ kāra bas rudhira antra mālā balaṃbine huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ sura bhakṣi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ gṛhṇa gṛhṇa subhadra sapta tāla gata bhujaṃga sarbaṃ bā tarjaya tarjaya huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ śyāma devi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ākaḍḍhya ākaḍḍhya bajra bhadra huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ hrīṃ hrīṃ mahā bhairaba huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ haya karṇeṇ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ jṇauṃ jṇauṃ birupākṣi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ kha khānane huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ kṣmāṃ kṣmāṃ mahā bale huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ cakra bege huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ hāṃ hāṃ ratna bajra huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ṣaṇḍa rohe huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ hīṃ hīṃ haya grība huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ ṣauṇḍini huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ hūṃ hūṃ ākāśa garbha huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ cakra varmiṇi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ kilili heruka huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ subīre huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ sili sili padma narteśvari huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ mahā bale huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ hili hili bairocani huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ cakra vartini huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ dhili dhili bajra satva huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
oṃ mahā bīrye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ
Taking up a rosary of great conch,
Recite the secret mantra according to the rite.
Having practiced at the four times,
Do recitation and meditation one-pointedly.
Having swiftly accomplished the yoga,
Through the sequence of accomplishing the secret mantra,
Having made oneself endowed with the mudrā,
The mantrin should always make offerings.
The mantrin, having entered the maṇḍala,
Meditates on the maṇḍala of body, speech and mind.
Through union with one's own deity
The supreme maṇḍala is accomplished.
Through union with the deities of the maṇḍala
All accomplishments are fully granted.

From the Highest of the Highest of the Tantra of Discourse, the sequence of the rite of the maṇḍala of the three wheels: this is the tenth chapter.

Then, furthermore, I shall explain
The supreme and excellent division of the families.
Through the sequence of joining the places and the rest,
They are distinguished by the order of the directions.
The great union, supreme of all:
The lord of yogins is fully accomplished.
Emerging from the vajra body,
It is the maṇḍala, the excellent maṇḍala.

Then the Blessed Ones, all the tathāgatas, and also all the heroes and yoginīs, gathered and came before the Buddha, the Blessed One, the lord of great bliss, the great Vajradhara.

Wide-eyed, dancing with joy,
In a sequence gathered up with speed,
With the right foot and the left
Stepping forth before him,
With eyes thus changed to wrath,
Raising their brows and rolling them,
They who go to the unborn mudrā
Bowed with the mudrā of the eyes,

and they urged the great Vajrasattva in many ways and made their request, and they spoke of the maṇḍala in which all the great yoginīs delight:

"The reality of the Blessed One
And the supreme maṇḍala are beautiful.
The vajra of great delight
Is all the buddhas, great bliss.
The great bliss of play
Today I have found.
a la la ho
The great bliss of all the tathāgatas
Is supremely delighted."
By singing these songs,
Existence is wholly and decisively turned back.
The great and pure tathāgata
Is contemplated in the maṇḍala.

Then the Blessed One, Great Bliss, Vajrasattva, reflected and opened his eyes wide, and with a dance that revolves around the four quarters he raised his brows.

Gazing ahead and with his steps
Whirling about in proud disdain:
Body, speech and mind are the vajra itself;
Reality abides in this.
The maṇḍala and the name of the maṇḍala
Abide in the mind of vajra wisdom.
Joining oneself without conceptual thought
Is the supreme vajra maṇḍala.
The full disk of the moon
Is the vajra of great delight.

Like the white of a painter,
Such is this supreme mind of awakening.
Like a fire stirred by the wind,
Blue and blazing in various hues,
Itself beautiful with a half moon,
It abides in the vajra maṇḍala.
The enchanting maṇḍala of eight parts,
Together with a blazing sun:
That maṇḍala, the vajra jewel,
Brings about the accomplishment of supreme bliss.
A lotus of eight petals, open,
Its center pervaded by anthers:
From the lotus, the lotus itself,
The maṇḍala of joy arises.
When the supreme maṇḍala is seen,
Just like a blazing fire,
In the karma vajra maṇḍala
All actions are accomplished.
In this way those infinite ones
Should be fully visualized in the maṇḍala.
Whatever emanates, wherever and in whatever,
Has arisen from Vajrasattva.
The nature of all is Samantabhadra,
Pervading the whole realm of space.
Upon the supreme ground of the sphere of space,
Meditate on a palace of many stories.
In the very center of the palace of many stories,
Visualize five maṇḍalas.
On beautiful multicolored lotuses,
Arrange the deities each in their place.
In the center of the central maṇḍala
Meditate on Heruka,
With the body color and positions explained before,
Abiding in bliss with Vārāhī.

In the east, in Pullīramalaya, are Khaṇḍakapāla and Pracaṇḍā. In the north, in Jālandhara, are Mahākaṅkāla and Caṇḍākṣī. In the west, in Oḍiyāna, are Kaṅkāla and Prabhāvatī. In the south, in Arbuda, are Vikaṭadaṃṣṭra and Mahānāsā.

In the center of the eastern maṇḍala of that central maṇḍala, in Godāvarī, are Suravairin and Vīramatī. In the east, in Rāmeśvara, are Amitābha and Kharvarī. In the north, in Devīkoṭa, are Vajraprabha and Laṅkeśvarī. In the west, in Mālava, are Vajradeha and Drumacchāyā. In the south, in Kāmarūpa, are Aṅkurika and Airāvatī.

In the center of the northern maṇḍala, in Oḍra, are Vajrajaṭila and Mahābhairavā. In the east, in Triśakuni, are Mahāvīra and Vāyuvegā. In the north, in Kosala, are Vajra Hūṃkāra and Surābhakṣī. In the west, in Kaliṅga, are Subhadra and Śyāmādevī. In the south, in Lampāka, are Vajrabhadra and Subhadrā.

In the center of the western maṇḍala, in Kāñcī, are Mahābhairava and Hayakarṇā. In the east, in Himālaya, are Virūpākṣa and Khagānanā. In the north, in Pretapurī, are Mahābala and Cakravegā. In the west, in Gṛhadevatā, are Vajraratna and Khaṇḍarohā. In the south, in Saurāṣṭra, are Hayagrīva and Śauṇḍinī.

In the center of the southern maṇḍala, in Suvarṇadvīpa, are Ākāśagarbha and Cakravarmiṇī. In the east, in Nagara, are Śrī Heruka and Suvīrā. In the north, in Sindhu, are Padmanarteśvara and Mahābalā. In the west, in Maru, are Vairocana and Cakravartinī. In the south, in Kulatā, are Vajrasattva and Mahāvīryā.

The heroes Khaṇḍakapāla and the rest, and Pracaṇḍā and the rest, all of them, are of various forms, three-faced, six-armed and three-eyed, standing with the right leg extended, and sealed with the five mudrās. The goddesses are naked, with their hair unbound. Holding a skull cup, a khaṭvāṅga, a hand drum, a curved knife, a vajra and a bell, the heroes embrace them and are entwined by the two thighs of the goddesses, intoxicated with the taste of the joy of great delight.

At the four corners of the heroes' central maṇḍala,
Upon vases,
Skull vessels filled to the brim
With the mind of awakening should be placed.
At the corners of the other maṇḍalas
Place both the vases and the skulls.

In the intermediate quarters are the four goddesses, Ḍākinī and the rest, of half form, sovereign ladies. They are three-faced and three-eyed, standing in the posture with the right leg extended, clad in the directions, with their hair unbound, and they firmly bear the five mudrās.

Encircled all around by a garland of vajras,
Outside the five maṇḍalas,
Draw various blazing maṇḍalas.

All the heroes and yoginīs are seated on seats of hungry ghosts upon multicolored lotuses. At the eastern of the four gates is Kākāsyā, with wrathful eyes, glassy eyes and a wrathful face. She holds a curved knife, a head, a skull cup, a khaṭvāṅga, a bell and a hand drum. She is three-eyed and three-faced, with her hair unbound, naked, multicolored, standing with the right leg extended, seated on a hungry ghost and a multicolored lotus. She abides in the center of a blazing maṇḍala and is adorned with the five mudrās. Ulūkāsyā, Śvānāsyā and Śūkarāsyā are just like Kākāsyā in color, arms, posture and seat. With the faces of a donkey, a camel, a goat and a cat, they devour with great ferocity.

Those from Yamadāhī to Yamamathanī are sovereign ladies of the dance. They are three-faced, six-armed and three-eyed, standing with the right leg extended, clad in the directions, with their hair unbound, trampling a multicolored lotus and a hungry ghost, blessed by the five mudrās. They hold a skull cup and a khaṭvāṅga, carry a head and a curved knife, and hold a hand drum, a vajra and a spear. Their fangs are bared, and they are terrifying. All of them wear garlands of human skulls as ornaments.

They have crown ornaments of garlands of skulls,
And have Vajrasattva as their crown ornament.
Their diadems are made of garlands of vajras,
And they have ornaments made of vajras.
A crossed vajra and a half moon
Press upon their heads and adorn them.
Just as with the color of the Blessed One,
So are the face color and arms of Vajravārāhī,
And her mudrās and posture as well.
She is naked, with her hair unbound,
Adorned with a girdle of bone pieces.
But the upper face of Vajravārāhī
Is white,
With fangs bared.

As for the sequence of the sites,
From the sequence of the Abhidhāna in a Hundred Thousand Lines,
The chapter on the maṇḍala of the yoginīs
Was taught by Vajrasattva.
Elsewhere, with the Vajraḍāka
And with the first chapter
Of the Abhidhāna Tantra there is certainly no contradiction.

This is the rite of the maṇḍala of pride,
The great union whose nature is the five of the fivefold,
The rite of the vow (saṃvara), concealed and secret,
Of all the ḍākinīs without exception,
Thus, as in the section of the teaching elsewhere.

Vases of many kinds,
Various vases in accordance with the rite,
Made of gold and of silver,
Beautified with various cloths,
And adorned with garlands of flowers,
Filled with the five nectars,
Endowed with the five kinds of medicine,
Together with the five jewels,
And beautified with the five grains:
Having set sixteen vases
In the directions and intermediate directions of the maṇḍala,
One should worship the ḍākinīs in various ways,
With song and dance and great bliss,
Clothed only in the directions, with hair unbound,
With the sounds of various music;
And with garlands of various pretas
One should encircle the outside of the maṇḍala.

Outside that, in the middle
Of the enclosing rim, are the ḍākinīs,
Encircled on the outside by a girdle,
And the terrifying charnel grounds,
Beautified with many various things.

Having seen before one
The supreme maṇḍala of wisdom and commitment,
One should likewise worship the commitment of the Abhidhāna,
And confer empowerment in its proper order.

The wise one, having arranged them on the body,
Gathered them into one and dissolved them,
Should don both armors:
The heroes and the yoginīs,
Abiding in the aggregates, elements and sense sources.

Having meditated on all the wheels,
One should set them in the aggregates, elements and sense sources,
And in body, speech and mind;
One should set the wisdom being in the heart.
With various pleasing things
One should worship with offerings to the commitment deity.
One should emanate countless
Activities of wisdom recitation.
Having taken a rosary of human bone,
And resting in equipoise, one should recite.
There, to the wheel of wisdom,
The wise one should offer worship, praise and recitation.
For the benefit of sentient beings
The yogin should make aspirations.
The teachings of the Great Vehicle
Are quickly accomplished by the yogin.

oṃ vajra he he ru ru kaṃ śrīḍākinījālasambaraṃ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ hrīḥ ha ha huṃ hūṃ phaṭ These are the heart and near-heart mantras.

oṃ vajravairocanīye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ sarvabuddhaḍākinīye vajravarṇanīye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā These are the heart and near-heart mantras of the goddess.

oṃ khaṇḍakapālina kara kara huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ pracaṇḍe huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ mahākaṅkāla kāru kuru huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ caṇḍākṣi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ kaṅkāla bandha bandha huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ prabhavati huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ vikaṭādaṃṣṭīṇi trāsaya trāsaya huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ mahānāse huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ suravairiṇi kṣobhaya kṣobhaya huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ vīramati huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ atābha hrauṃ hrauṃ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ kharvari huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ vajraprabha hraḥ hraḥ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ laṅkeśvari huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ vajradeha pheṃ pheṃ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ vajradrumacchāye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ aṅkurika phaṭ phaṭ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ airāvati huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ vajrajāṭila daha daha huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ mahābhairavi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ mahāvīra paca paca huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ vāyuvege huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ vajra hūṃ kṣara bhakṣa bhakṣa vasa rudhira antramālā valaṃvini huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ surabhakṣi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ suvtadra gṛhṇa gṛhṇa saptapātālagataṃ bhujaṃgasrvaṃ vātārjjaya huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ śyamadevi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ vajrabhadra ākaḍḍhya ākaddhya huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ subhadre huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ mahābhairava hrīṃ hrīṃ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ hayakarṇeṇa huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ virūpākṣi jṇauṃ jṇauṃ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ khagānane huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ mahāpala kṣmāṃ kṣmāṃ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ cakravege huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ rantavajra hāṃ hāṃ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ khaṇḍarohe huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ hayagrīva hīṃ hīṃ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ śauṇḍini huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ ākāśagarabha hūṃ hūṃ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ cakravarmiṇa huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ heruka kili kili huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ suvīre huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ padmanarteśvara sili sili huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ mahābale huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ vairocana hili hili huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ cakravartin huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ vajrasatva dhili dhili huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ mahāvirye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ ḍākini huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ lāme huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ lāme huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ khaṇḍarohe huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ rūpiṇi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ kākāsye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ ulūkāsye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ śvānāsye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ sūkarāsye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ yamadāḍhi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ yamadūti huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ yamadaṃṣṭri huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ yamamathani huṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā

By this sequence, just as it is,
For the benefit of others, the one who keeps the commitments
Should worship, meditate and recite.
Pacification and enrichment increase,
And one obtains the pleasing accomplishments.
The supreme and sublime one among the classes of the egg-born
Grants all desires through union.
If one performs the rites just as they are,
There is no doubt that they will be accomplished.
The sublime and supreme accomplishment of union
Is found from the words of the guru.
Moreover, the sites of the yoginīs,
Supreme in the sequence of the lineage,
Or else the earlier accomplishments,
Are shown in dreams.
Moreover,
Attainment of the state of the guru
Is fully bestowed by the yoginīs.
This secret of the supreme commitment,
This hidden thing, should not be taught.
The mind of the yoginīs
And the mind of the buddhas are supreme;
The secret heart of the ḍākinīs
Is the supreme of yogic union.

This is the eleventh chapter, on the sequence of the sites of accomplishment, from the Highest of the Highest of the Abhidhāna Tantra.

Next I shall explain the practice
That accomplishes every desired aim,
Higher even than the highest:
The Saṃvara of the net of the ḍākinīs.
Vārāhī, the great sovereign lady,
Is well accomplished by the yoginīs.
This is the yoga of all,
The condensed heart of yoga.

One should draw an eight-petaled lotus,
Of various colors, supreme and sublime.
On its center, together with the anthers,
Is a seat that is completely pure in every way.
In its middle is a lunar disc,
Likewise pure and stainless.
On it are the vowels and consonants,
And in their middle it is adorned with vaṃ.
In its middle the goddess arises:
Vajravārāhī, the yoginī,
Four-faced and with twelve arms,
Three-eyed, with contorted faces,
Seated in the half cross-legged posture,
Beautifully adorned with dance,
Clothed in the directions, with hair unbound.
With the face of the half-female Lord (Ardhanārīśvara),
She bears a form that is white and red,
Adorned with a crown of skulls.
She holds a vajra and a bell in her hands,
Encircled by a coil of lotuses,
And on her forehead a mudrā blazes.
With two hands she wears a human skin;
She holds a skull cup and a khaṭvāṅga,
And holds a noose and a supreme hook,
A damaru, a curved knife, and the head of Brahmā.
Her faces are four:
With blue, yellow and green faces
The goddess is adorned.
Her faces bare their fangs,
Adorned with a half moon,
Marked by a crown ornament of crossed vajras.
She fully bestows all accomplishments.
Sealed with the six mudrās,
The goddess is adorned with a girdle of bone pieces,
A belt of bells that resounds,
Beautifully adorned with anklets,
Beautified with armlets,
On her forehead a garland of vajras,
Wearing mudrās marked with vajras,
Her body beautiful and captivating.

On the eastern petal is the Ḍākinī,
Lion-faced and terrifying.
To the left is the one with the face of a pig,
The goddess Lāmā, greatly terrifying.
In the west is the elephant-faced goddess,
Khaṇḍarohā, the sovereign lady.
In the south, in the guise of a goddess,
Is Rūpiṇī, with the face of a horse.
Three-eyed, of various forms,
They hold a skull cup and a khaṭvāṅga,
And carry a head and a curved knife in their hands.
Naked, with hair unbound,
They are beautified with dance as well.
On the backs of pretas, in the half cross-legged posture,
With contorted faces, they are very terrifying.
Bhairava and Kālarātri
Are pressed down beneath the feet of the goddesses.
In the four intermediate directions, beginning with that of Fire,
One should set vessels of bodhicitta and the rest
Upon vases.

In the color of conch, jasmine and the moon,
One should draw a six-spoked wheel.
In its middle the wise one should draw
The four of the mātṛkā family:
Māmakī and Locanā,
The goddess Tārā and Pāṇḍaravāsinī.
Blue, white and red
Is the goddess, sovereign of the vajra family.
With faces white, blue and red,
The goddess is of the family of permanence.
Green, white and red,
The goddess is of the karma family.
Red, yellow and green,
The goddess is of the lotus family.
Three-eyed, with hair unbound,
They wear the supreme garment of the directions,
Complete with every ornament,
Adorned with the six mudrās.
They hold a skull cup and a khaṭvāṅga,
And their own emblem is the fourth:
Vajra, wheel, crossed vajra,
And red lotus, supreme and sublime.
All of them likewise hold a bell,
Encircled by a coil of skulls.
Treading down the four māras with their feet,
Their faces laughing with dance and song,
They sit in the middle of a moon on a multicolored lotus,
Fully bestowing the accomplishments of the families.
In their hands they carry a head and a damaru,
Adorned with a vajra curved knife,
With crown ornaments of garlands of skulls,
And adorned with garlands of heads.

On all the corners
One should set the sequence of the sites and the rest.
In the east, on the six-spoked wheel,
One should set the goddesses in their corresponding order.

On the first corner, in Pullīramalaya, is Pracaṇḍā. Second, in Jālandhara, is Caṇḍākṣī. Third, in Oḍḍiyāna, is Prabhāvatī. Fourth, in Arbuda, is Mahānāsā. Fifth, in Godāvarī, is Vīramatī. Sixth, in Rāmeśvara, is Kharvarī.

Second, on the six-spoked wheel of the north: first, in Devīkoṭa, is Laṅkeśvarī. Second, in Mālava, is Drumacchāyā. Third, in Kāmarūpa, is Airāvatī. Fourth, in Oḍra, is Mahābhairavā. Fifth, in Triśakuni, is Vāyuvegā. Sixth, in Kosala, is Surābhakṣī.

On the eastern spoke of the six spokes of the third wheel, the western one, in Kaliṅga, is Śyāmādevī. Second, in Lampāka, is Subhadrā. Third, in Kāñcī, is Hayakarṇā. Fourth, in Himālaya, is Khagānanā. Fifth, in Pretapurī, is Cakravegā. Sixth, in Gṛhadevatā, is Khaṇḍarohā.

On the eastern spoke of the six spokes of the fourth wheel, the southern one, in Saurāṣṭra, is Śauṇḍinī. Second, in Suvarṇadvīpa, is Cakravarmiṇī. Third, in Nagara, is Suvīrā. Fourth, in Sindhu, is Mahābalā. Fifth, in Maru, is Cakravartinī. Sixth, in Kulatā, is Mahāvīryā.

The first are white and green,
With skull cup and khaṭvāṅga,
Wheel, damaru and curved knife.
The second, of the north, are blue and yellow,
With vajra, skull cup and khaṭvāṅga,
Damaru and curved knife.
The third, of the west, are red and yellow,
With skull cup and khaṭvāṅga,
Lotus, damaru and curved knife.
The goddesses of the fourth, of the south,
Yellow and red, hold a crossed vajra,
A damaru, a curved knife, a skull cup,
And a khaṭvāṅga as well.
All of these goddesses, too,
Are half-female sovereign ladies.
Three-eyed and four-armed,
Naked, with hair unbound,
In the half cross-legged posture, with dance,
They stand with their feet on the backs of pretas,
With crown ornaments of garlands of skulls,
Adorned with the five mudrās,
With anklets and girdles,
And crown ornaments marked with vajras.

Behind that wheel,
In the east, one should encircle it with a garland of vajras;
In the north, a garland of wheels;
In the west, a garland of crossed vajras;
In the south, a garland of lotuses.

In the middle it is adorned with a garland of vajras.
On all the corners
One should draw crossed vajras evenly.
Behind that, the wise one should encircle it
With a garland of various pretas.

At the gates of the outer maṇḍala
And on its four corners,
One should draw and set the forms
Of the assembled ḍākinīs, while resting in equipoise.
The crow-faced one, Kākāsyā, is set in the east,
Blue and red and captivating.
The owl-faced one, Ulūkāsyā, is set at the northern gate,
Green and red and beautiful.
The dog-faced one, Śvānāsyā, at the western gate,
Is yellow and smoke-colored and very fierce.
The pig-faced one, Sūkarāsyā, set in the south,
Has a form of green and blue.
Three-eyed, with contorted faces, very fierce,
They wear the sublime garment of the directions.
With eyes askew and hideous forms,
They stand with the right leg extended.
They sit in the middle of a sun on a multicolored lotus,
Adorned with the five mudrās,
With crown ornaments of garlands of skulls,
Of various forms and captivating,
Naked, with hair unbound, terrifying.

Yamadāhī, buffalo-faced,
Abides in the direction of Fire.
Yamadūtī, donkey-faced,
Abides in the direction of Nairṛti.
Yamadaṃṣṭrī, camel-faced, the fierce one,
Abides in the direction of Wind.
Yamamathanī is horse-faced.
These are intoxicated with passion,
With crown ornaments of garlands of skulls,
Of various forms, captivating ladies,
Naked, with hair unbound,
In the half cross-legged posture, dancing,
Their faces baring their fangs,
Three-eyed, with swaying bodies.
They sit in the middle of a sun on a multicolored lotus,
Intoxicated with hideous passion,
All of them seated upon pretas.
In the maṇḍalas of the wheel and the rest
They hold skull cups and khaṭvāṅgas,
And carry curved knives and heads;
In their right hands their damarus
Sound forth a most terrifying noise.
Their manner is to encircle the outside.

Furthermore, with many kinds
Of song and dance as offerings,
And with the various sublime commitment substances,
With flowers, incense and lamps,
With scents, garlands and ointments,
With all kinds of divine garments,
And with goddesses with cymbals, supreme and sublime,
One should worship the outer yoginīs.
In its center, before one, without limit,
Everywhere filled with a retinue
That fills the whole element of space,
One should also worship the supreme and sublime Vārāhī
And the fierce yoginīs.
These supreme things are secret and hidden;
They are not to be told to anyone at all.

Outside, with a garland of pretas,
The nature of the supreme maṇḍala,
Its magical display, should be set out;
One should set it out in sequence, just as it is.

The nails and teeth are Pracaṇḍā. The hair of the head and body is Caṇḍākṣī. The skin and its impurities are Prabhāvatī. The flesh is Mahānāsā. The sinews are Vīramatī. The bones are Kharvarī. The kidneys are Laṅkeśvarī. The heart is Drumacchāyā. The eyes are Airāvatī. The bile is Mahābhairavā. The lungs are Vāyuvegā. The intestines are Surābhakṣī. The colon is Śyāmādevī. The stomach is the goddess Subhadrā. The bladder is Hayakarṇā. The hairline is Khagānanā. The phlegm is Cakravegā. The pus is Khaṇḍarohā. The blood is Śauṇḍinī. The menstrual fluid is Cakravarmiṇī. The fat is Suvīrā. The tears are Mahābalā. The saliva is Cakravartinī. The mucus is Mahāvīryā.

Pullīramalaya: Pracaṇḍā at the head. Jālandhara: Caṇḍākṣī at the crown. Oḍḍiyāna: Prabhāvatī at the right ear. Arbuda: Mahānāsā at the back of the head. Godāvarī: Vīramatī at the left ear. Rāmeśvara: Kharvarī between the eyebrows. Devīkoṭa: Laṅkeśvarī at the eye. Mālava: Drumacchāyā at the two shoulders. Kāmarūpa: Airāvatī at the two eyes. Oḍra: Mahābhairavā at the two breasts. Triśakuni: Vāyuvegā at the navel. Kosala: Surābhakṣī at the tip of the nose. Kaliṅga: Śyāmādevī at the mouth. Lampāka: Subhadrā at the throat. Kāñcī: Hayakarṇā at the heart. Himālaya: Khagānanā at the groin. Pretapurī: Cakravegā at the sexual organ. Gṛhadevatā: Khaṇḍarohā at the anus. Saurāṣṭra: Śauṇḍinī at the two thighs. Suvarṇadvīpa: Cakravarmiṇī at the two calves. Nagara: Vīramatī at the toes. Sindhu: Mahābalā at the insteps. Maru: Cakravartinī at the two big toes. Kulatā: Mahāvīryā at the two knees.

The earth element is Pātanī, the water element is Māraṇī, the fire element is Ākarṣaṇī, and the wind element is Narteśvarī. The space element is Padmajālinī.

The aggregate of form is Vairocana, the aggregate of feeling is Vajrasūrya, the aggregate of perception is Narteśvara, the aggregate of formations is Vajrarāga, and the aggregate of consciousness is Vajrasattva. The self of all the tathāgatas is Vajra Śrī Heruka.

The eye is Delusion Vajra, the ear is Aversion Vajra, the nose is Avarice Vajra, speech is Desire Vajra, the body is Envy Vajra, and all the sense sources are Lordship Vajra. The mind is Akṣobhya Vajra, speech is Amitābha Vajra, and the body is Vairocana Vajra.

Setting these out
Is seeing the wheel of wisdom.
If one abides in this practice,
One thereby meditates on everything.
Empowerment, worship, commitment
And meditation are made equal, as the essence.
To the wisdom being at its heart
One offers the essence of the nectar of wisdom.
The commitments of body, speech and mind,
Mind and what is not mind, should be well examined.
Through the practice of recitation and meditation,
Having abandoned all conceptual thought,
By the recitation of the supreme commitment
The supreme king of secret mantras is accomplished as well.

oṃ vairocanīye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ sarvabuddhaḍākinīye vajravarṇanīye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā

One should join them properly with praises and with the names.
Having joined the secret mantras of Pracaṇḍā and the rest,
Endowed with hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ,
One should perform the supreme recitation.
Having fully completed the recitation,
One should also drink the supreme drink of peace.
This is the supreme commitment of sameness,
In which all phenomena are completely the same.

Therefore, having meditated on it, one is completely pure:
The supreme state of the thirty-seven,
Selflessness, the Dharma and the path,
Fully abide in the supreme maṇḍala.
The yoginī of the perfection of wisdom,
Having rested from the work of yoga
And abiding in the signless state,
Relies evenly on the signless.
Phenomena do not exist and are without signs;
Neither non-dharma nor that which has dharmas exists.
Dependently arisen phenomena, too, are equal to space,
Without entity and without signs.
If one abides here on that very ground,
One sees with the eye of wisdom.
The wheels of the sites and the rest, too,
One certainly sees as equal to motes in a sunbeam.
Even a portion of the tip of a hair split a hundred thousand times
One fully realizes and sees.

Heruka, dwelling in the center of the bhaga
Together with the yoginī,
Is the cakravartin, fully united;
One sets Kākāsyā and the rest outside.
Glorious Heruka, the lord,
Is surrounded by the four ḍākinīs.
Acting as lords to one another,
The union of Vajrasattva and the rest
Is accomplished in common with the yoginīs.
The three unions are the supreme commitment.
The great lord of the three realms
Has not been seen to engage in conduct.
If it is not accomplished, one becomes a cakravartin,
A king who is lord of the three realms.
If it is accomplished, then, as a great yogin,
One attains lordship over the three existences.
Likewise, through the practice of the rite,
One should eat one who has died by violence.
Those who commit the deeds of immediate retribution
The wise one should summon and consecrate;
Otherwise the accomplishment becomes fearsome.
The fruit of livelihood is as one wishes.
The practice of slaughtering livestock
Should be done after asking the yoginīs;
It is the practice of preparing the divine food offering.
With mother and sister together,
Likewise the supreme and the middling;
Otherwise, together with a daughter,
The two organs joined in union
Is the accomplishment of supreme sameness.
Through the practice of one's own deity
One should enjoy the five sense pleasures.
Having meditated on oneself as Vārāhī,
And having placed the threatening finger at the navel,
The ḍākinīs and the rest abide in the wheel.
Having made the goddess deeply pleased,
And having entered the nine gates,
Having pierced the lotus of the heart,
Merely by striking the yoginī
One should drink the supreme wound-born.
If one eats what that striking produces,
One becomes a buddha, and not otherwise.

This is the twelfth chapter, teaching the sequence of signs for accomplishing the sites of the yoginīs, from the Highest of the Highest of the Abhidhāna Tantra.

Next, I shall explain further
The rite of the yogin heroes
And the arising of the sites of the yoginīs;
I shall explain the assembly.
All the heroes and yoginīs
Are not apart from the guru's command.
All the ḍākinīs
Will certainly bestow excellence.
Nine squares are in the center,
And outside one should draw sixteen.
In the very middle of the central square
One should draw a most beautiful lotus,
A multicolored lotus of eight petals,
With its center and its anthers.
In its middle, having visualized a moon,
One should arrange ā and the rest;
Having made the vowels and consonants even,
One should place hūṃ within.
All-pervading through Vajrasattva,
One should meditate on Heruka,
With five faces and ten arms,
Fully adorned by Vārāhī,
White and blue and likewise red,
Green, and yellow on top,
Three-eyed, with terrifying faces,
Standing in the posture with the right leg extended.
Bhairava and Kālarātri,
Greatly intoxicated, he presses beneath his feet.
With a crown ornament of a garland of skulls,
Beautified by bearing the half moon,
With a diadem of crossed vajras,
His matted locks are bound on his head as a crown.
Sealed with the six mudrās,
His body adorned with vajra ornaments,
Equally endowed with vajra and bell,
He is the supreme bliss of great bliss.
He holds a trident and a damaru,
Likewise a vajra and a curved knife,
A skull cup, a khaṭvāṅga, the head of Brahmā,
An axe and a noose
In his right and left hands.
He wears a human skin as his garment.
His two legs evenly entwined,
He is joined in union with Vārāhī.
She has his color, arms and posture;
Her hair unbound, naked,
She wears a tiger skin as a garment,
Has a girdle of ornaments of bone pieces,
And a garland of fierce skulls,
Intoxicated with the passion of compassion.

On the eastern petal is the Ḍākinī,
In the north Lāmā, the ḍākinī,
In the west is Khaṇḍarohā,
In the south Rūpiṇī, the ḍākinī:
Black, dark blue, red and pale yellow,
Wearing the garment of the directions, standing with the right leg extended,
With crown ornaments of garlands of skulls,
Hair unbound, and nondual.
On all the corners
One should also place vessels of bodhicitta.
They hold a skull cup, a khaṭvāṅga, a bell,
A damaru, a curved knife and a head,
Three-faced, three-eyed, with contorted faces,
Intoxicated with the passion of fierce passion.
The Blessed One, having gazed upon them with bliss,
Is attached to great bliss.
Wearing a necklace of heads,
Heruka is to be meditated upon.
Thus in the very middle of the central square
One should meditate on these supreme ones too.

In the eastern square of it, in Pullīramalaya, are Khaṇḍakapāla and Pracaṇḍā, yellow. In the north, in Jālandhara, are Mahākaṅkāla and Caṇḍākṣī, blue. In the west, in Oḍḍiyāna, are Kaṅkāla and Prabhāvatī, white. In the south, in Arbuda, are Vikaṭadaṃṣṭrin and Mahānāsā, green. In the direction of Fire, in Godāvarī, are Suravairin and Vīramatī, red. In Nairṛti, in Rāmeśvara, are Amitābha and Kharvarī, lotus-colored. In the direction of Wind, in Devīkoṭa, are Vajraprabha and Laṅkeśvarī, smoky blue. In Īśāna, in Mālava, are Vajradeha and Drumacchāyā, green and like pearl. Holding a vajra and a bell in their hands, they embrace.

Then, in the east of the second square, in Kāmarūpa, are Aṅkurika and Airāvatī, red. In the north, in Oḍra, are Vajrajaṭila and Mahābhairavā, green. In the west, in Triśakuni, are Mahāvīra and Vāyuvegā, blue. In the south, in Kosala, are Vajrahūṃkāra and Surābhakṣī, white. In the direction of Fire, in Kaliṅga, are Subhadra and Śyāmādevī, sky-colored and pale yellow. In Nairṛti, in Lampāka, are Vajrabhadra and Subhadrā, red. In the direction of Wind, in Kāñcī, are Mahābhairava and Hayakarṇā, yellow. In Īśāna, in Himālaya, are Virūpākṣa and Khagānanā, blue. Holding a lotus and a bell, they embrace.

In the east of the third square, in Pretapurī, are Mahābala and Cakravegā, sky-colored and pale yellow.

In the north, in Gṛhadevatā, are Vajraprabha and Khaṇḍarohā, yellow. In the west, in Saurāṣṭra, are Hayagrīva and Śauṇḍinī, red. In the south, in Suvarṇadvīpa, are Ākāśagarbha and Cakravarmiṇī, blue. In the direction of Fire, in Nagara, are Śrī Heruka and Suvīrā, smoke-colored. In Nairṛti, in Sindhu, are Padmanarteśvara and Mahābalā, green. In the direction of Wind, in Maru, are Vairocana and Cakravartinī, blue. In Īśāna, in Kulatā, are Vajrasattva and Mahāvīryā, white. Holding a wheel and a bell, they embrace.

Moreover, they hold a skull cup, a khaṭvāṅga, a damaru, a curved knife and a head; they are six-armed and three-faced, the faces being taught in accordance with the oral instructions; three-eyed, they stand with the right leg extended.

They are adorned with the five mudrās,
Wear a tiger skin as a garment,
And are adorned with garlands of heads,
With diadems of garlands of skulls.
Vajra garland, lotus and wheel
Adorn their foreheads in corresponding order.
The goddesses, hair unbound and naked,
Entwine them closely with their two thighs.
As is the means, so is the wisdom,
Well pervaded by the mudrā of the emblems.

Four-cornered, with four gates,
Adorned with four porticoes,
Adorned with nets and half nets,
Adorned with silk banners and garlands:
Having laid it out in the form of a wheel,
One should examine carefully how it may be beautiful.
Thus, for the outer wheel,
One should set out the gatekeepers as well.
In the intermediate directions and the corners
The wise one should draw the goddesses.
Having heard many rites,
I have gathered them into one with effort.

At the eastern gate is Kākāsyā,
With three faces and six arms;
Her right and left faces
Are the faces of a crow and a heron;
She is squat, with a hideous face.
At the northern gate, with owlet,
Owl and cat she is beautiful;
In her right and left faces
She moves her tongues, terrifying in form.
In the west is the one called Bhavagī,
With the faces of a dog and a lion;
In her left and right faces
She moves her tongues, most beautiful.
In the south, the earth-holding goddess
Is pig-faced, with the form of a buffalo;
Her right and left faces
Are deep, fierce and terrifying.
The first is called Kākāsyā,
The second likewise the owl,
The third is the dog-faced one,
And the fourth is the pig itself.
Faces of blue, smoky and white;
Green, yellow and red;
Faces of yellow, red and blue;
White, blue and green in color.

First, from Fire up to Īśāna,
Are Yamadāhī, Yamadūtī,
Yamadaṃṣṭrī and Yamamathanī:
Red and yellow and green,
Blue and yellow and green,
Smoky, yellow and green,
White and yellow and green.
They have three faces and six arms,
Are three-eyed and stand with the right leg extended,
Wear the garment of the directions, with hanging bellies,
Their hair unbound,
Sealed with the five mudrās.
And all the wheels
Rest upon pretas,
Seated on multicolored lotuses
And disks of sun and moon.
In their hands they carry skull cup and khaṭvāṅga,
Noose, hook and damaru,
Head and curved knife,
Adorned with garlands of skulls,
Intoxicated with joy and delight.
Their passionate faces
Turn terrifying, glaring eyes.

Then Yamadāhī and the rest are at the gates,
And Kākāsyā and the rest in the intermediate directions,
Placed in union with their own means.
Having meditated on the stage of union,
All the heroes of the wheel, too,
Are just like Vajraḍāka;
All of them, following the former,
And all the ḍākinīs and the rest likewise.
Just as the guru commands,
Just as one wishes, so too are the sites.
According to the distinctions of mind,
One should meditate on the maṇḍala of the yoginīs.

By arranging various vases,
It is fully adorned with skull cups;
Skull cups filled with milk
Should be placed upon the vases.
Surrounding it on the outside
Dwell various ḍākinīs;
Accompanied by song and dance,
They emanate various kinds of music.
With flowers, incense and lamps,
Scents and sublime garments,
Various commitment substances, the food of the flavors,
Various edibles and liquor, one should worship.
The various supreme and sublime maṇḍalas
Are surrounded on the outside by pretas.
One should meditate on entity and non-entity
In accordance with the command of the guru's oral instructions.
The vajra, lotus ring and wheel
Abide fully between the four corners;
The intermediate directions are marked with half vajras.
In the front center of the vajra pillars
The squares should be furnished with vajras.
In the supreme rite of the variegated,
One should set out the sites and the rest in order,
And likewise the elements and aggregates;
One should set out the six sense sources too.
One should meditate on the wisdom vajra.
Through union with body, speech and mind,
When one sees the wheel of wisdom,
One should worship with argha and water for the feet.
With the various supreme and sublime commitments,
Through the distinctions of their variety,
One should worship with māṃsarakta (flesh and blood).
Through joining the two armors,
The supreme and sublime mudrā of wisdom,
If one meditates on the single taste without remainder,
One meditates on the wheel of wisdom.
Through the distinction of touch,
The wise one joins in the three unions.
Through meditation, the meaning of complete purity:
The wise one, the thirty-seven;
Through the practice of wisdom recitation,
One should eat with the supreme of the three commitments.
Through the practice of emanation and gathering,
One should meditate on the meditation of recitation.
At the four times, the holder of commitments,
Wise in the union of the sites, should keep guard.
Everything that is seen on the earth
Is all the form of glorious Heruka.
Purifying the existence of the six destinies,
One accomplishes immeasurable benefit for sentient beings.
All having entered, through the practice of entering,
One should make all enter here.
This is the certain practice;
One will attain certain accomplishment.
With rosaries of human bone
One should recite the supreme commitment.
In accordance with the sequence of the rite of the gathering,
The yogin should recite the supreme mantra.

oṃ śrīvajra he he ru ru kaṃ ḍākinījālasambara hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ hrīḥ h ha hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ These are the heart and near-heart mantras.

oṃ vajravairocanīye hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ svāhā oṃ sarvabuddhaḍākinī vajravarṇanīye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ These are the heart and near-heart mantras of Vārāhī.

oṃ kara kara khaṇḍakapāli huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ praca caṇḍe huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ kuru kuru matā kadkāla huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ caṇḍapakṣi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ bandha bandha kadkāla huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ prabhavati huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ trāsaya trāsaya vikaṭa daṃṣkiṇi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ mahānāse huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ kṣobhaya kṣobhaya suravairiṇi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vīrama huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ hrauṃ hrauṃ amitābhe huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ kharvari huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ hraḥ hraḥ vajraprabhe huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ laṅkeśvari huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ pheṃ pheṃ vajradehe huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ drumacchāye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ phaṭ phaṭ adkuriko huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ airāvati huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ daha daha vajrajaṭila huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ mahābhairavi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ paca paca mahāvīra huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ vāyuvege huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ bhakṣa bhakṣa vajrahūṃkāra vasarudhira antramālāvalaṃvini huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ surabhakṣi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ gṛhna gṛhna subhadra saptapātālagata bhujaṃga sarvaṃ pātarjjaya huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ śyāmadevi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ ākaṭḍhya ākaṭḍhya vajrabhadre huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ subhadre huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ subhadre huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ hrāṃ hrāṃ mahābhairavi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ hayakarṇeṇ huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ jṇauṃ jṇauṃ virūpākṣi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ khagānane huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ kṣmāṃ kṣmāṃ mahābale huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ cakravege huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ hāṃ hāṃ ratnavajra huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ khaṇḍarohe huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ hīṃ hīṃ hayagriva huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ śauṇḍini huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ hūṃ hūṃ ākāśagarbhe huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ cakravarmiṇi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ kili kili heruka huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ suvīre huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ sali sili padmanarteśvare huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ mahābale huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ hili hili vairocani huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ cakravartini huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ dhili dhili vajrasatva huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ mahāvīrye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ ḍākini huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ lāme huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ khaṇḍarohe huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ rūpiṇi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ kākāsye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ ulākāsye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ śvānāsye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ sūkarāsye huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ yamadāḍhi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ yamadaṃṣṭi huṃ hūṃ phaṭ oṃ yamamathana huṃ hūṃ phaṭ

By merely reciting with the rite of yoga,
The vajra is summoned, and from its place
It is stirred up and violently shaken.
Light blazes from the body, there is laughter,
Its eyes blaze,
There are gentle words and blazing;
By the yogin's mere recitation
The entry of the vajra is seen.
The mantrin, having recited in meditative equipoise,
Having taken up the conduct of the commitment,
Having held firm the vital breath and its exertion (prāṇāyāma),
And having uttered the illuminating hūṃ,
Should emanate into the sphere of phenomena.
To benefit all sentient beings
And to tame those hard to tame,
One emanates the qualities of the buddhas before oneself.
Having seen that state of the wrathful one,
One should gather it back into the three realms.
Having made it enter body, speech, and mind,
One should practice the conduct upon the earth.
When the yogin not in meditative equipoise,
Moistened with compassion,
Looks upon all sentient beings,
The three worlds of existence are Heruka.
Whatever the mantrin eats,
Having meditated on it as the supreme commitment,
All is the victorious Lord of Dharma;
He abides fully in sameness.
When the one who keeps the commitment rises,
Recollecting the worship of the guru,
He should bathe with the water of the mind of awakening.
Abiding with the lovely mudrā,
Having blessed it into the state
Of the wrathful ones, the buddhas, the heroes,
The yoginīs, and the ḍākinīs,
This is taught as the delightful offering.
For the maṇḍala that pervades the expanse of space
Aspirations are to be made.
When, by the yoga that follows immediately,
One sees the moving and the unmoving,
The Blessed One is pure by intrinsic nature,
And intrinsic nature too is made insubstantial.
Sentient beings are pure by intrinsic nature,
And existence is made supreme.
Having blessed the unsurpassed Vajravārāhī
As the mudrā,
Through the union with one's own deity
One should examine everything in this way.

This is the thirteenth chapter, from the supreme Higher Abhidhāna Tantra, teaching the arrangement of the grid and the gathering of the sacred places.

Then I shall explain the secret.
Since the sacred places and the gatherings are supreme,
The intrinsic nature of all accomplishments,
Blended in sameness, becomes the essence.
In equipoise with a nondual understanding,
Within the three wheels endowed with their colors,
In the center of a square maṇḍala,
Is the supreme wheel of the twenty-four.
In its center is a great lotus,
A variegated lotus, beautifully lovely,
Eight-petaled, together with its hub,
Adorned all about with anthers.
The square has four gates,
Ornamented with four archways,
Adorned with garlands and half-garlands,
Fully endowed with all the characteristics.
In the center of a sun disc
Is Vajrasattva, the great bliss.
From the vowels and consonants, having melted,
One should meditate on the five wisdoms.
Through their complete increase
One meditates on Vajra Heruka.
With the great blazing fire of the end of time
He utterly slays all who are hard to tame.
Bhairava and Kālarātri
He tramples beneath his feet.
The fangs of his mouths are bared,
His three eyes are fearsome and utterly terrifying.
From his body, which is blue,
He emanates a body, blue and blazing.
He wears a crown of a garland of skulls,
Blessed by the five buddhas;
He wears a crown of crossed vajras,
Beautified by a half moon.
Six-faced, the hero is hideous,
With a body graceful and laughing,
Fierce and terrifying.
Sealed with the six mudrās,
His body adorned with various ornaments,
He is united with Vārāhī,
Embracing her nondually with the thighs.
He stands with the right leg extended,
He abides with the left leg extended.
He makes ha ha and makes hūṃ hūṃ,
He makes hi hi and is terrifying,
He makes hūṃ hūṃ and has phaṭ phaṭ,
He utters the great sound of aṭṭāṭṭā.
Blue, yellow, and likewise red,
Green, and likewise white;
The upper face is greatly fierce.
Enjoying the vicious as his food,
Holding vajra and bell together,
He presses the breasts of the goddesses.
He wears a garment of a fierce beast's skin,
And drinks the fresh, dripping blood.
He holds a skull cup and a khaṭvāṅga,
A spear, a curved knife, a hook,
A noose, and a head.
Vicious and fierce, with a ḍamaru,
And with the mudrā of a blazing spear,
His forehead is beautifully adorned.
With such hands and body color he abides.
His hair loose, he is naked:
One should meditate on a mudrā such as this.
Adorned with a girdle of skull pieces,
Vārāhī too should be meditated on like this.
The yogin, having abandoned doubt,
Through uniting method and wisdom,
Through the bliss of compassionate passion,
Arises for the sake of sentient beings.
Moistened with great compassion,
Through the enjoyment of the union of space,
Through the bliss that is the essence of great compassion,
Having purified the existence of the six classes of beings,
He accomplishes the supreme benefit of sentient beings.
Arisen from the wisdom of emptiness,
In the ground free of conceptual thought,
Without intrinsic nature and supremely subtle,
Free of drop and sound,
Having seen the commitment of sameness,
Which is supreme, outer and inner,
The essence, the wisdom, and the commitment,
In the center of a moon disc,
Having seen there the hand emblem and the color,
The moon, the vajra, and the seed syllable,
Here one should meditate on that essence,
And examine it in the yogas of approach.
In the sequence of the Ḍākinī and the rest,
One should set them on the lotus petals.
In the east one should set the Ḍākinī,
Likewise in the north Lāmā,
In the west is Khaṇḍarohā,
Likewise in the south Rūpiṇī.
Blue, green, red,
And yellow; likewise the four
Have distorted faces and three fierce eyes,
Loose hair, and are clad in the directions.
All their ornaments are complete,
They are sealed with the five mudrās;
A skull cup and a khaṭvāṅga
Rest against the left shoulder;
A ḍamaru, a curved knife, and a head
Are the supreme commitment of the ḍākinīs.
Filled with the water of the mind of awakening,
A skull cup is to be placed on top of the vase.
As for setting out the wheel:
The yoginīs accomplished before,
In the sequence of blessing and so on,
Are in the order of east and north.

In Pullīramalaya are Khaṇḍakapāla and Pracaṇḍā, pale yellow like the sky. In Jālandhara are Mahākaṅkāla and Caṇḍākṣī, yellow in color. In Oḍiyāna are Kaṅkāla and Prabhāvatī, green in color. In Arbuda are Vikaṭadaṃṣṭrin and Mahānāsā, pale yellow like the sky. In Godāvarī are Suravairin and Vīramatī, yellow in color. In Rāmeśvara are Amitābha and Kharvarī, green in color. In Devīkoṭa are Vajraprabha and Laṅkeśvarī, pale yellow like the sky. In Mālava are Vajradeha and Drumacchāyā, yellow in color. In Kāmarūpa are Aṅkurika and Airāvatī, green in color. In Oḍra are Vajrajaṭila and Mahābhairavā, pale yellow like the sky. In Triśakuni are Mahābhairava and Vāyuvegā, yellow in color. In Kosala are Vajrahūṃkāra and Surābhakṣī, green in color. In Kaliṅga are Subhadra and Śyāmādevī, pale yellow like the sky. In Lampāka are Vajrabhadra and Subhadrā, yellow in color. In Kāñcī are Mahābhairava and Hayakarṇā, green in color.
